#5060. Binance hack launderers mint homemade Elon Musk, animal memecoins to move stolen funds: Elliptic

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Six throwaway tokens helped obfuscate stolen NEAR as trading proceeds, repeating a tactic used after the 2025 Bybit hack.

North Korea's money launderers are spinning up throwaway memecoins to help obfuscate funds stolen in the $6.4 billion Binance hack, according to blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

In a new report released Monday, Elliptic said about 117,000 NEAR, worth roughly $430,000 at the time, were routed into six newly created memecoins created between Nov. 5 and Nov. 7.

Like many other memecoins, the tokens feature animal mascots and one sporting an expletive ticker. Another features Elon Musk under an "Elon 2028 President" banner. Elliptic added that there was no suggestion that Musk himself was linked to it.

The launderers can then use decentralized exchange liquidity pools to separate wallets holding stolen funds from wallets taking in proceeds. One set of wallets will buy the memecoins with stolen NEAR, while another sells the token into the pool and collects NEAR in return.

"At no point do the stolen funds move directly from a buying wallet to a collecting wallet, because the pool sits in between," Elliptic wrote. "To a casual observer, the collecting wallets look like ordinary traders who have sold a token at a profit."

The tokens have all since been abandoned, with Elliptic adding that much of the NEAR collected went into stablecoins or cross-chain bridges.

Elliptic noted that this same tactic was used following the 2025 Bybit hack, with launderers moving around $24 million through a homemade token paired with a stablecoin on Tron.

In the Binance case, the firm also found evidence of a similar approach on Solana using existing tokens that had low liquidity.

The transfers are still public and traceable on the blockchain. Elliptic said the additional swaps are only meant to be hurdles to slow investigators down, giving the launderers a little extra time to move funds before their wallets get flagged.

The amounts moved through the six NEAR tokens are still small compared to the 11.8 million NEAR worth around $42.7 million at current prices.

This follows Elliptic's update from Friday, which tracked roughly $124 million in stolen assets converted into bitcoin. Binance's earlier incident report said that the attack had been in the works for months and involved malicious developer software, a fake job interview, and compromised AI tooling.

The exchange said the FBI linked the attack to North Korea.

#5057. 7 Best Sex Blankets for Mess-Free Fun, According to Sex Experts

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Let's face it: Any kind of sex is likely going to involve fluids. And while that's totally normal, nothing kills the mood quite like dealing with that dreaded wet spot on your bed sheets immediately after having sex. There is a way to avoid that annoyance completely, though—I'm talking about sex blankets.

Sex blankets let you get as messy as you want during sex without worrying about the cleanup afterward. Aside from the fact that they help protect your sheets from lube, ejaculate, period blood, and more, they can also make sex even hotter by creating a more relaxed environment. "The more comfortable our environment is, the less things we’re worried about [and] the easier it is to relax and release into pleasure," says Jenn Gunsaullus, PhD, a sociologist, intimacy and relationship coach, and author of From Madness to Mindfulness: Reinventing Sex for Women. "A sex blanket can help make us more comfortable, and reduce any stress about being judged, making a mess, or messing up something nice."

While sex blankets come in a variety of styles and sizes, the best ones have a few characteristics in common. Generally, you'll want to look for a blanket that's waterproof and designed to protect your mattress topper/couch/floor/countertop from any fluids. It's also best to look for a product that's machine-washable and a great size for your needs. Ahead, sex therapists and sex educators unpack the top traits to look for in a sex blanket.

What really sets a sex blanket aside from a regular one is its capacity to reduce mess. So, you'll need a waterproof product, and it'll help if the blanket is also absorbent, says Goody Howard, a Dallas-based sex educator. "The best sex blanket is going to be one that has good absorption, not just something that is waterproof and doesn't allow things to get wet." She recommends looking for keywords like 'charcoal' and 'microfiber' in the product description. This will let you know it's actually absorbent instead of just a plastic-lined piece of fabric, Howard says.

Size is also an important feature to consider. Some sex blankets don’t cover the entirety of a bed, so if you’re looking to cover a lot of surface area, you'll want to opt for a larger blanket like the Liberator Fascinator Throw. On the other hand, a smaller sex blanket, like the Playdrop Sex Mat, might be more travel-friendly and easier to toss in the wash. It all depends on your needs.

If you plan on keeping your sex blanket out in plain sight, you probably don't want it to stick out like a sore thumb. "Keep in mind where the blanket will live in your space so that the color or design matches the area," Howard says. A product that matches your sheets, comforter, or pillows might also make the whole experience of using a sex blanket feel sexier and more natural.

For the past year, Women’s Health editors vetted the bestselling, top-rated sex blankets on Amazon and beyond. After consulting two sex experts, we personally tested each blanket by pouring a cup of water on it and watching how the material responded. We also sent blankets home with our team of anonymous reviewers for further testing, and evaluated thousands of customer reviews and ratings to narrow down the best sex blankets for a wide range of needs and preferences.

Plus, it has a protective inner liner that locks in moisture, ensuring your bed stays spotless and dry.

According to testers, it does take a while to dry out after a wash, but other than that they love this blanket. One even said that it dramatically enhanced their sex life because it's much more romantic than the towels they were using before purchasing this throw blanket.

It also comes in nine colors and three sizes, with the smallest (30 x 40 in.) costing less than $20.

Howard is also a fan. "It is a pet blanket, but hear me out: It serves the same purpose, comes in a great variety of colors, and is ridiculously affordable," she says. You would never even know this was made for your furry friend.

This blanket from Catalonia looks and feels like one of those lightweight throws that are used for decor and thrown on the foot of a bed. Available in several neutral colors, it easily doubles as a daily throw, so you never have to get ready because you technically always are. Each blanket is reversible, so if one side gets a little too wet and wild, you can easily flip it to the other.

Our tester says she's never had an issue with this blanket not protecting her sheets from fluids. However, she did note that although it's comfortable to lay on top of, it's a bit too crunchy to lay underneath.

Don't want the bulk of another blanket on your bed? This one is more like a flat sheet, so it can cover a lot of surface area. In fact, the California King size is the largest blanket on this list, so if you have a big bed (or just want to be extra safe), this is an ideal option.

But to be so lightweight, this blanket doesn’t have an absorbency layer. Instead, it’s made of a thin, raincoat-like material so any liquids will pool on top of it. Our testers didn’t mind this feature, but just noted that this might make the blanket better suited for camping sex than indoor intimacy. That said, the material is designed with a silky feel, so it's comfortable enough to use for regular ol’ sleeping, too. Then, whenever the mood strikes, your bed is already set up.

Love the feel of natural fibers? Look no further. This circular sex blanket from Dame is made of ultra-soft cotton with an internal waterproof layer. It also comes in three colors (sage, oat, and copper) and with a travel-ready pouch for easy and discreet storage.

Overall, our testers appreciated the lightweight and airy feel of this material and that it was absorbent, rather than water-resistant. And while some liked the circular shape of this blanket, others would’ve preferred if the blanket were square- or rectangular-shaped like the other options on this list.

For on-the-go intimacy, your best bet is this travel-sized reversible blanket with a waterproof core. As with the Dame Thro, the cotton waffle weave is absorbent, not water-resistant, so liquids won’t pool on top of the blanket. And while it’s not entirely made of natural fibers (it’s 57 percent polyester), our testers liked how soft the material felt. They also liked the vibrant color combination of lilac and orange. Plus, it folds flat into a storage bag, so it’s easy to stash in your backseat for car sex.

The only potential downside is the price. It’s slightly more expensive than other sex blankets—both similar size and larger.

#5056. Coinbase and PNC Bank expand partnership with USDC payments for corporate clients

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The pilot program will automatically convert incoming stablecoin payments into U.S. dollars, following the launch of bitcoin trading for PNC's private banking customers last year.

PNC Bank is expanding its partnership with crypto exchange Coinbase Coinbase Global, Inc. (COIN) to let corporate and institutional clients receive payments in USDC, according to an announcement on Monday. The service is initially being offered through a pilot program for eligible customers.

The bank will automatically convert incoming USDC payments into U.S. dollars at a 1-to-1 rate before depositing the funds into clients' designated PNC accounts. The pilot uses Coinbase Payment APIs for stablecoin transactions, alongside PNC's banking and treasury management services. PNC said it would complement the faster-payment options already available to its corporate clients.

PNC first announced its partnership with Coinbase in July 2025, agreeing to use the exchange's Crypto-as-a-Service platform while providing select banking services to Coinbase. In December, the bank began offering spot bitcoin trading to eligible private banking clients, allowing them to buy and sell bitcoin directly through their existing investment accounts.

Coinbase has also been working with other banks on stablecoin payments. In September, it expanded its partnership with Citi to connect traditional fiat payments with stablecoins. The arrangement allows Coinbase customers to use Citi's Virtual Account Wallet to receive and manage fiat payments, with automatic conversion into stablecoins. Citi's institutional clients can also accept stablecoin payments through its Spring platform, using Coinbase's infrastructure to convert them into fiat for settlement.

Earlier this year, Coinbase also partnered with payments firm Moov to bring stablecoin acceptance, settlement, and real-time funding capabilities to systems used by more than 1,000 community banks and credit unions using Coinbase's Payments API and custodial wallets.

#5054. Crypto Card Provider Gnosis Pay Says Hackers Accessed Customer Data

Crypto Card Provider Gnosis Pay Says Hackers Accessed Customer Data

Crypto card provider Gnosis Pay said Friday that hackers accessed customer information through the system behind its own branded cards, the latest in a string of security incidents across the industry.

The company’s monitoring systems detected unauthorized access on Nov. 3, and containment began within two hours, Gnosis Pay said in a blog post. It has since replaced the compromised security credentials and fixed the vulnerability. Customer funds weren’t affected, the company said.

The breach was limited to Gnosis Pay’s consumer business, whose platform was already scheduled for retirement on Dec. 20. Its enterprise platform, which powers white-label card programs for fintech firms, wallets and neobanks, wasn’t affected.

Gnosis Pay didn’t say how many customers were affected or what data was exposed, but said it emailed each affected customer with details of what was taken from their account.

“While funds were not affected, the security of personal information is just as important and we take full responsibility,” the company wrote on X.

Gnosis Pay said it has stepped up monitoring of card activity and wallets for fraud, and has made 3D Secure, an extra verification step, mandatory for all online card payments until the consumer platform is retired. It has reported the breach to its partners, its card issuer and data protection authorities, and is arranging specialist monitoring for signs that the data is misused.

We had a data breach affecting the system powering Gnosis Pay-branded cards (our B2C business line) that resulted in the exposure of certain customer information.

Everyone affected was sent an email today with full information.

We deeply regret that this happened. While funds…

— Gnosis Pay 💳 (@gnosispay) November 6, 2026

The company urged customers to be wary of unexpected messages about their cards, even ones that include real personal details or purchases. It said it will never ask for seed phrases, PINs or full card numbers, or ask users to move funds.

It also told customers to be careful about sharing their address or details of their crypto holdings, both online and offline.

That last warning reflects a growing threat. Physical “wrench attacks” on crypto holders, including home invasions and kidnappings, were on pace for a record year after more than $30 million was stolen in the first half of 2026, according to blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis.

The disclosure follows several other recent examples of breaches in crypto security. Beyond the record-setting $6.4 billion hack of the world’s largest crypto exchange in Binance, rival trading platform Coinbase said last week that a breach at analytics vendor Amplitude exposed some customer contact data.

Last month, hardware wallet maker Ledger said all confirmed cases in a suspected $87 million drain involved devices bought from CryptoBilis, a Southeast Asian reseller, and urged recent buyers to move their funds to new devices.

After testing more than 50 sweatpants over the past two years, I can confidently say today’s best sweatpants for women are no longer reserved for bed rotting on lazy Sundays. #5051. StoneX sees 97% upside for Bullish as $4.2 billion Equiniti deal opens tokenization market

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

StoneX says Bullish's pending $4.2 billion Equiniti acquisition will make it transfer agent of record for about 30% of the S&P 500 and half the FTSE 100.

StoneX Financial on Monday initiated coverage of Bullish with a Buy rating and a $42 price target, arguing that the exchange is set to become a "toll booth" for tokenized shares if its Equiniti acquisition deal follows through.

Bullish shares were trading above $22 in early morning trading, up 2.9% on the day, according to The Block's BLSH price page. StoneX's target implies roughly 97% upside from that level, although the stock is down nearly 50% over six months.

In a note to clients, StoneX analyst Mark Palmer said the Equiniti acquisition will give Bullish a transfer agency business serving nearly 3,000 issuers, 15,000 corporate clients, and 20 million shareholders once completed.

"Every big market structure shift needs a toll booth, and BLSH is buying one," Palmer wrote. "When the deal closes, BLSH will own the transfer agent of record for ~30% of the S&P 500 and half the FTSE 100, thereby commanding the point at which a share stops being a database entry and becomes a token."

The $4.2 billion Equiniti deal includes $1.85 billion in assumed debt and about $2.35 billion in Bullish shares. Equiniti processes $500 billion in annual payments, according to the note.

Palmer said Bullish could capture a bigger share of a market that is projected to grow astronomically in the coming years. Tokenized equities total about $2.1 billion onchain, against $151 trillion in global equity value. The broader tokenized asset market, meanwhile, could reach $9.4 trillion by 2030, according to projections cited in the note.

On revenue, the exchange's existing business provides a solid base, Palmer noted. Bullish's second-quarter adjusted revenue rose 62% year over year to $92.6 million. Its subscription, services and other revenue also reached a record $62.7 million, per the note.

StoneX flagged several risks to its investment thesis, including an unforeseen delay or failure of the Equiniti deal. The firm also pointed to slower tokenization adoption and competition from Nasdaq, DTCC, NYSE, Securitize, and Superstate.

Meanwhile, Bullish appointed former New York Stock Exchange President Stacey Cunningham as its president. The appointment comes as the company awaits regulatory approval for its Equiniti acquisition and expands its issuer-sponsored tokenization business across Solana, Aptos, and Ethereum.

#5049. Binance France emails announce closure of its users’ accounts on Dec. 31

Any assets left on the exchange will be charged monthly fees starting on Nov. 29, company says.

Binance France is closing up shop in the country. Customers have been notified by email during early November, according to media reports.

All accounts will be closed on Dec. 31, and any assets left on the exchange will be charged monthly fees starting on Nov. 29, Cryptoast, Crypto Briefing and other media reported on Saturday, citing emails sent to users by Binance.

On that date, accounts still maintaining a balance will pay a monthly maintenance fee of 0.025% of the balance, or a minimum of 10 USDC, a stablecoin tracking the US dollar.

To avoid fees, withdrawals must be completed by Nov. 29, the exchange said.

A Binance email cited by Cryptoast said: “Due to the orderly cessation of activities, your Binance France account will close as of December 31, 2026. If you have not withdrawn your crypto-assets as of December 31, 2026, this message constitutes a notification of our intention to consider your account inactive, in accordance with our Terms of Use. This can result in the conversion, transfer or other processing of your crypto-assets.”

Related: Binance’s EU services face scrutiny over licensing exemption: Report

Cointelegraph reported in late June that Binance had notified European Union users access to key services would be restricted after the exchange failed to secure Markets in Crypto-Assets (MiCA) regulatory authorization from a member state before a July 1 deadline.

On Sunday, a Binance spokesperson responded to a Cointelegraph query, saying, “Following the end of the MiCA transition period, Binance is progressively restricting certain services for affected users, in line with the schedule previously communicated to them.”

Though asked specifically about reports similar emails had also been sent to users in Spain and Belgium, the spokesperson made no mention of them.

According to media reports, Binance’s global client base counts at least 300 million customers, while the app was downloaded more than 4 million times in the EU last year.

#5047. Ethereum Foundation, Vitalik Buterin sued over firing after ‘suicide pledge’ dispute

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The complaint challenges Fulton’s dismissal and quotes a recorded Buterin conversation about handling internal dissent.

A former Ethereum Foundation employee is suing the organization and members of its leadership, alleging she was unlawfully terminated after challenging an employee mandate.

Kayleen Fulton, known by the moniker "Nixo," filed the complaint on Nov. 4 in Colorado. The defendants include the Ethereum Foundation, its Singapore affiliate, executive Bastian Aue and Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin.

The basis of the lawsuit stems from Fulton's dismissal on April 6 and a dispute over the Foundation's "EF Mandate" that outlines its updated mission and principles. Fulton's suit also alleges disability discrimination and interference with medical leave rights coinciding with a cancer diagnosis she disclosed to the EF around the same time.

The Block reached out to Fulton and the Ethereum Foundation, but did not receive a reply from either before publication.

According to the complaint, employees were given the choice to affirm their commitment to the mandate's direction or leave. However, the filing acknowledges that Aue said the seppuku reference was "not meant to be taken literally" and later clarified that the commitment had "nothing to do with literal seppuku."

Fulton and another colleague, Trent Van Epps, organized a letter endorsing the EF's direction while objecting to some of the conditions of the mandate, which ultimately garnered 53 signatures. The complaint alleges that because of this pushback, Fulton and Van Epps were unlawfully terminated.

The filing also quotes a May 1 conversation with Buterin that Fulton partially recorded. It does not state whether Buterin knew or consented to the conversation being recorded, but notes that it took place in Norway, where one-party consent to recording is legal.

The complaint quotes Buterin as saying that "if you reward making a stink with outcomes," employees could use that as a way to influence the Foundation.

Fulton said she was offered a year's salary under a separation agreement, but alleges she signed the release under duress, received no payments, and later rescinded it.

She is seeking an unspecified amount in damages and lost pay.

#5044. US Sanctions 45 More Targets Tied to Alleged Crypto Scam Empire Prince Group

US Sanctions 45 More Targets Tied to Alleged Crypto Scam Empire Prince Group

The U.S. Treasury Department has sanctioned four individuals and 41 entities that it says support Prince Group, the Cambodian conglomerate accused of running scam compounds that use trafficked workers to defraud victims through crypto investment and romance scams.

The new designations, announced on Thursday by the Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC), target the offshore companies, nominee owners and administrators that Treasury says have helped Prince Group executives conceal assets and evade earlier sanctions imposed in October 2025 and June 2026.

"Cyber scam syndicates continue to steal billions of dollars from American victims while relying on sophisticated international business networks to launder their illicit proceeds," Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said.

Among those sanctioned is Qiu Yong, who Treasury said controls a network of British Virgin Islands and Hong Kong companies on behalf of Prince Group, including money lenders and wine businesses, and who has worked for a company controlled by Prince Group chairman Chen Zhi. His personal assistant, Zhao Mengyao, was also designated.

OFAC also targeted two people it said are holding property for, or running companies with, Prince Group executive Chen Bo, who was sanctioned in June. They include his wife, He Yujing, to whom Chen Bo transferred ownership of his Tokyo mansion after the October 2025 sanctions, according to Treasury, and associate Visal Pang, who controls 11 Cambodian companies spanning construction, logistics, a hotel and a "super app." Eleven companies still controlled by Chen Bo were also sanctioned, including Prince International Airlines and CB General Insurance.

Further entities in Singapore, Mauritius and Malaysia were sanctioned for links to Prince Group associate Dai An, along with a Singapore media company owned by alleged Prince Group finance chief Thet Li.

Separately, OFAC designated Chui Kok Wa, a Malaysian national based in Madagascar, who Treasury said led a network of scammers that used crypto and other technologies to defraud Americans of more than $400,000.

The U.S. is seeking the forfeiture of about $14 billion in Bitcoin tied to Prince Group, while UK authorities have frozen the London penthouse of Thet Li, Decrypt reported last month. Prince Group has denied the allegations.

Chen Zhi himself was arrested in Cambodia in January along with two other Chinese nationals and extradited to China, where state media described him as the head of a major cross-border gambling and fraud syndicate, the BBC reported. Cambodia, which had revoked his citizenship the month before, also placed the group's Prince Bank into liquidation.

Thursday's action was coordinated with the FBI, the Justice Department's Scam Center Strike Force, which has reported $580 million in crypto seizures and freezes, and the U.S. Attorney's Office for the District of Alaska, with help from authorities in the British Virgin Islands and the UK, according to Treasury.

Interpol declared scam compound networks a global threat last year, citing their use of human trafficking, online fraud and coerced labor. In September, the Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network linked about $12.7 billion in reported financial activity to suspected crypto investment scams, largely run by criminal organizations operating scam compounds in Southeast Asia.

The UN Office on Drugs and Crime estimated in July that scam operations across East and Southeast Asia, Australia and New Zealand cost victims between $88.3 billion and $114.1 billion in 2025, much of it through crypto investment fraud run from compounds holding people from at least 80 countries.

"She is looking to end the current Wild West-style lack of oversight in this area, with corrupt insiders profiting," the aide said.

#5040. Vitalik ‘pivots’ ETH to store of value, crypto suffers after US midterms: Hodler’s Digest

Vitalik Buterin wants Ethereum to embrace a store of value narrative, upsetting Bitcoin maxis. The crypto industry faces an uncertain future following the US elections.

Ethereum creator Vitalik Buterin says the network should promote ETH as a store of value, rather than as an asset that derives value from burning fees.

He argued in a thread that idea of “ETH as fee-burning token never was as strong as it seemed” and that optimizing for that would negatively impact Ethereum’s “decentralization and capture resistance.”

The 2021 EIP-1559 fee burning upgrade flipped Ethereum into deflationary mode for a time when fee revenue was sky high, leading to supporters, including the Bankless podcasters, touting Ethereum as “ultrasound money.”

But due to design choices, fees have kept falling along with revenue, making that narrative unsustainable. From January to October this year median transaction fees fell from 35 cents to 3.5 cents, even as the number of daily transactions increased by more than half.

Some Bitcoiners, who hold BTC’s store of value status sacred, were scathing about Buterin’s thread. Held Media founder Dan Held derided Buterin’s comments as, “ETH trying to pivot to Bitcoin’s SoV narrative after all of its previous narratives collapsed. Again.” He argued it was “wild that you think a protocol with a constantly changing monetary policy (just like fiat) can be a ‘sound money.’” However, proponents note that Ethereum has not changed its monetary policy since The Merge in 2022, and each change has reduced, rather than increased, issuance.

Ethereum maxis welcomed the protocol’s creator setting a clear narrative direction. MetaLeX attorney Gabriel Shapiro called it a “nature is healing moment” from Buterin, and said, “I think on fundamentals ETH can be a much better SoV than both ZEC and BTC.”

Even Solana co-founder Anatoly Yakovenko said Buterin was “absolutely right that you can’t focus on fees as the product” although he then took a subtle dig at Ethereum’s “product.”

Despite spending $176 million across 100 seats — including $64 million trying and failing to keep Sherrod Brown out of the Senate — the influence of crypto super PACS on the election appeared to be minimal. Or was it?

Even though the Democrats now control both the Senate and the House of Representatives a Fairshake spokeman told Crypto In America the super PAC had spent $154.5 million across 93 races and backed the winning candidate in all but seven races. That’s a 92% success rate.

Stand With Crypto notes that a majority of legislators in both houses is now pro-crypto, with 50 of 100 senators designated “pro-crypto” and only 33 “anti-crypto,” while 241 congress people support the industry, and only 106 actively hate it.

However the anti-crypto army will slither into positions of power, with Brown likely to join the Senate Agriculture Committee and Elizabeth Warren tipped to chair the Senate Banking Committee.

“The next Congress will bring a fundamentally different environment for crypto in Washington, and the industry should expect significantly more scrutiny,” said Kyle Bligen, executive director at the Decentralization Research Center.Democrats are already reportedly considering expanding proposals from Representative Maxine Waters into a broader market structure bill. There is still an outside chance the CLARITY Act could be resurrected, but only if it has much stronger ethics provisions aimed at President Donald Trump’s alleged corruption.

“A sitting president’s family launching memecoins and a stablecoin is a real governance problem,” Fidelity Digital founder Hadley Stern told Magazine. “If I’d done that at Fidelity, I’d have been walked out the same afternoon.”

The TRUMP memecoin lost a third of its value this week, and was the biggest loser in the top 100 cryptocurrencies.

The election result saw AI related stocks crash and Bitcoin fell below $76,000 where it remains.

Binance reports that North Korean hackers first breached its systems back in June, when an employee downloaded a tampered Homebrew installation while attempting to install an AI coding tool. While the exchange quickly detected the incident, the hackers had already obtained information about employees working on Binance’s wallet infrastructure.

In September, attackers posing as recruiters targeted another software engineer and tricked the employee into downloading a malicious file disguised as a coding assignment.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used the compromised computer and an internal AI tool to gain access to two additional engineers’ systems to deploy malware. They then gained access to the exchange’s internal wallet-signing service, enabling them to steal billions.

“This wasn’t one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign,” Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said in a Tuesday post on X.

Ethereum Foundation researcher Justin Drake is targeting December 2028 to complete Ethereum’s post-quantum and post-AI transition, a year earlier than the foundation’s previously announced quantum-resistance deadline.

Speaking at Devcon 8 in Mumbai on Tuesday, Drake outlined the accelerated timeline. “Our hope is that by December 2028 we’ll be able to wrap up the full post-quantum and post-AI transition,” Drake said, adding that developers have 24 months to deliver three additional network upgrades.

The Ethereum Foundation’s September roadmap established December 2029 as its deadline for achieving quantum resistance.

However the roadmap is currently on hold, with core developers delaying the Glamsterdam upgrade indefinitely until security concerns tied to eight smart contract invariants are resolved.

THORChain has ignored the FBI’s explicit warning not to process funds from the Binance hack, with the permissionless protocol restoring trading on most networks on November 4. The RUNE token gained 15% in 24 hours on the news that Bitcoin, Ethereum, Bitcoin Cash, Dogecoin, Litecoin, Tron and Zcash had been re-enabled. Avalanche, Base and XRP were already operating.

Chainalysis reported that THORChain had already processed more than $46 million from the hack in the first 72 hours after Binance was attacked, with validators making around $200,000 in fees according to the Wall Street Journal. More would have undoubtedly gone through, but trading on the cross chain swaps platform was halted automatically due to an unrelated Ethereum block reorganization.

Co-founder JPThor said the fact that the FBI warning had no effect on the DEX proved that THORChain was decentralized.

At the end of the week, Bitcoin (BTC) is down 4.5% to trade at $74,302, Ethereum (ETH) is down 6.3% to trade at $2,117 and XRP (XRP) is down 5% to $1.11. The total market cap is at $2.49 trillion according to CoinMarketCap.

The top three altcoin losers of the week are OFFICIAL TRUMP which was down 32.8%, Worldcoin (WLD) down 20.8% and Ethena (ENA) down 19%.

#5038. Bitcoin ETFs Bleed $430 Million as Tech Rout Tests Wall Street Buyers

Bitcoin ETFs Bleed $430 Million as Tech Rout Tests Wall Street Buyers

U.S. spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds lost $430.2 million to net withdrawals Thursday as a technology-stock selloff spread to crypto, with Fidelity and BlackRock leading the exits.

Fidelity's Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) posted $199.9 million in net outflows and BlackRock's iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) shed $105 million, according to Farside Investors. Together they accounted for about 71% of the day's withdrawals.

Grayscale's GBTC lost another $49.9 million, while Franklin Templeton's EZBC shed $32.9 million. No fund in Farside's table recorded positive net flows Thursday, although several reported no net movement.

The latest withdrawals brought this week's net outflow to $869.8 million, following a $643.2 million outflow last Friday. Wednesday's modest $74.6 million inflow offered a brief reprieve before Thursday's reversal.

Fidelity's swing was particularly sharp: FBTC attracted $236.7 million Wednesday, then surrendered nearly $200 million the next session. BlackRock, meanwhile, lost $201.2 million even on Wednesday's positive day, extending a five-session withdrawal streak since October 30 to roughly $1.04 billion.

ETF flows have often been treated as a gauge of demand from investors accessing Bitcoin through regular brokerage accounts. The latest figures show that access works both ways: the same familiar wrapper that makes buying easy also makes exiting straightforward.

Because ETFs trade on stock exchanges, investors can adjust their Bitcoin exposure without transferring coins to a crypto wallet. That convenience can also make the funds an easy place to cut risk during an equity selloff. Daily net flows capture shares created or redeemed at the fund level, not every trade between investors.

Thursday's redemptions arrived as the Nasdaq Composite plunged 5.5% and the S&P 500 fell about 4.4%. Chipmakers and data-center stocks led the decline as investors questioned the pace of artificial-intelligence spending and potential restrictions after the U.S. midterm elections.

Bitcoin also surrendered its post-election rebound, falling back toward $76,000 after trading above $80,000 Wednesday. Short liquidations had helped power that rally, making the subsequent reversal more painful for leveraged traders.

The ETF figures add another piece to that picture. Net flows measure creations minus redemptions of ETF shares; they cannot tell whether holders were raising cash to cover losses elsewhere, trimming Bitcoin exposure, or rebalancing portfolios.

#5035. Paraguay Protests Over Bank Lockout Intensify as Impeachment Vote Nears

Paraguay Protests Over Bank Lockout Intensify as Impeachment Vote Nears

Thousands of Paraguayans marched through Asunción this week demanding their money back after Ueno Bank, a digital lender, locked about 3 million customers out of their accounts, according.

That is about 60% of Paraguay’s adults. The bank also held $800 million to $850 million in public money, including state pension and health funds, according to the AP, so the fallout reaches the pension system.

Anger boiled over Thursday night outside Ueno’s headquarters in Asunción, where savers burned debit and credit cards. Paraguayo “Payo” Cubas, a former presidential candidate, tried to join and was booed, shoved, and forced out.

Protesters shouted “divisor” and “oportunista,” meaning divider and opportunist, accusing him of trying to profit politically from public rage, according to the same report.

Diario Hoy reported that protesters also blamed him for the Senate’s current makeup, saying Cubas put Zenaida Delgado and Javier Vera on his party’s legislative ticket, the slate of candidates voters choose from, and that both joined the government’s side soon after taking office.

President Santiago Peña, a shareholder in Ueno’s holding company until March 2025, submitted his resignation Thursday, but a joint session of Congress called to accept it adjourned without a quorum, according to La Nación. He then said he would face impeachment—a trial in Congress to remove a president—and defend himself.

The lower house had already voted 78-0 to open impeachment, and on Friday the Senate rejected his request for 72 extra hours to prepare his defense. The Senate planned a removal vote for Friday night, ABC Color reported, with Vice President Pedro Alliana next in line.

Horacio Cartes, the Colorado Party leader and Peña’s former political mentor, withdrew his support and backed the impeachment. “We will not be on the side of the plunder of public, private and retirees’ funds,” Cartes said.

Interior Minister Enrique Riera and central bank president Carlos Carvallo have resigned, and eight Ueno executives have been arrested, according to La Nación’s Friday update. Prosecutors also opened a criminal investigation into Peña’s possible responsibility for the bank’s irregularities, which he denies.

Police used water cannons and tear gas on marchers near Congress, and a new law requires most deposits to be returned within five business days, though protesters have not been placated.

The central bank says its deposit guarantee covers up to 228.3 million guaraníes per person, about $36,000, but as Decrypt previously reported, the first payouts will go only to accounts with up to 10 million guaraníes (around $1,600) and no debts to Ueno.

Bitcoiners, of course, rushed to use Paraguay’s banking crisis as an example on the benefits of decentralization and self custody, and why the “Bitcoin fixes this” motto is still relevant in today’s financial system. “Bitcoin adoption at a nation-state level can mitigate these types of situations,” said JAN3 founder Samson Mow. “If BTC is available to the people as a parallel rail, then systemic banking failures have far less impact.”

#5033. tBTC bridge partially resumes as $120M Aave reserve remains frozen

Threshold halted its tBTC bridge on Oct. 30 as operators addressed a critical Linux kernel vulnerability that could allow attackers to execute code on unpatched servers.

Threshold Network’s tBTC bridge has resumed onchain operations after a nine-day security pause, but an Aave reserve holding about $120 million in tBTC remains frozen to new deposits and borrowing against the token.

On Sunday, Threshold said the bridge’s underlying network was restarting as security-patched node operators reconnected. These independent operators collectively authorize Bitcoin movements and process deposits and redemptions.

Requests submitted before the shutdown are expected to be processed as more operators return, but the app will not accept new deposits, minting or redemptions until enough nodes are online. Threshold did not announce a restart date.

Threshold initially halted the bridge on Oct. 30 while operators addressed a critical Linux kernel vulnerability that could allow remote attackers to execute code on unpatched servers. The project said it found no evidence the flaw was exploited against tBTC nodes.

Aave froze its tBTC reserve on Ethereum Core on Oct. 31 to prevent additional exposure while redemptions were unavailable. The restriction stops new tBTC deposits and the token from adding borrowing power, while existing users can still withdraw and repay loans.

Threshold said roughly 4,320 BTC remains in bridge wallets against about 4,185 tBTC in circulation, with no unauthorized Bitcoin transfers or token minting detected. Aave risk provider LlamaRisk said it would propose restoring the reserve after Threshold’s operations return to normal.

#5028. Why a Hyperliquid Treasury Firm Wants to Become a US Futures Broker

Why a Hyperliquid Treasury Firm Wants to Become a US Futures Broker

Hyperliquid Strategies built its business around holding HYPE. Now it wants to help Americans trade on Hyperliquid, too.

The Nasdaq-listed treasury firm, which trades as PURR and is chaired by former Barclays CEO Bob Diamond, disclosed in its quarterly results Thursday that a subsidiary has applied to become a futures commission merchant, or FCM.

That’s the type of CFTC-registered broker that holds customer funds and handles futures orders. If approved, the unit would carry U.S. client accounts for permissioned markets built on Hyperliquid, letting Americans trade perpetual futures within a regulated framework.

Perpetual futures are derivatives with no expiration date that let traders take leveraged positions. Hyperliquid, a major onchain venue for them, still blocks U.S. users.

Its HIP-3 framework lets outside firms launch their own perpetual markets on Hyperliquid’s order book after staking HYPE. A new permissioned version, called HIP-3*, would let a regulated deployer run markets restricted to approved traders, with Hyperliquid acting as neutral infrastructure.

“We deployed $802 million this quarter and filed an application to become a futures commission merchant,” CEO David Schamis said. “Those two moves are the same bet.”

The firm held about 38.5 million HYPE at the end of September, worth roughly $3.5 billion, and more U.S. trading routed through Hyperliquid would likely mean more fees for the protocol, which uses fee revenue to buy back HYPE. An FCM would also give Hyperliquid Strategies a business of its own beyond holding tokens.

During the quarter, the firm spent $785 million buying 9.1 million HYPE and $17 million repurchasing 2.7 million of its own shares, funded largely by $911 million raised through stock sales. It reported $620.6 million in net income, driven almost entirely by $803 million in unrealized gains as HYPE rose from about $65 to $91.

It won’t be alone. Kraken parent Payward has said it plans to deploy HIP-3* markets through its CFTC-regulated Bitnomial exchange, with NinjaTrader Clearing serving as the FCM.

And last week, NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange agreed to team up with Trade[XYZ] to bring Hyperliquid-based perpetual futures to the U.S., sending HYPE up 13%. Trade[XYZ] already runs markets on Hyperliquid, including perpetual futures tied to the S&P 500.

Regulators must sign off on both the FCM registration and the permissioned HIP-3 markets it would serve, and the company has given no timeline.

#5023. Zcash devs propose another block time cut days after NU7 upgrade

The proposed reduction aims to shorten transaction confirmation times and improve the user experience for point-of-sale payments, exchange deposits and cross-chain swaps.

Zcash developers have proposed reducing the blockchain’s target block interval from 25 seconds to 20 seconds, seeking faster transaction confirmations days after activating the NU7 upgrade.

On Sunday, developers ValarDragon and Evan Forbes submitted a draft Zcash Improvement Proposal (ZIP) on the Zcash community forum, saying that they want the change to be included in the next community poll and considered for the upcoming NU7.1 network upgrade.

The reduction would shorten the expected wait for initial transaction confirmations. According to the proposal, this would improve the user experience for point-of-sale payments, exchange deposits and cross-chain swaps.

The developers said the change would not increase the network’s overall daily capacity for private transactions. The proposal would also preserve Zcash’s existing issuance schedule, despite blocks being produced more frequently.

The proposal comes just days after Zcash’s NU7 upgrade went live. On Friday, the NU7 upgrade was activated on the mainnet, cutting the network’s target block interval from 75 seconds to 25 seconds.

The upgrade also introduced a Network Sustainability Mechanism designed to recycle a portion of transaction fees into future mining rewards while preserving Zcash’s 21 million ZEC supply cap.

#5022. Greece Passes Crypto Tax Law With 10% Gains Levy, 15% on Staking and DeFi

Greece Passes Crypto Tax Law With 10% Gains Levy, 15% on Staking and DeFi

Greece's parliament passed a bill Thursday imposing a 10% capital-gains tax on cryptocurrency profits, marking a shift from a proposal discussed in October to legislation approved by lawmakers.

The measure also puts a 15% tax on income from staking, crypto lending and decentralized finance, or DeFi.

The legislation’s crypto provisions sit inside a wider Finance Ministry package dealing with debt servicing, financial oversight, and government accounting, with the new crypto income-tax rules scheduled for January 1, 2027.

Individual investors with annual net crypto gains of €500 ($557) or less will be exempt. Cross that threshold, though, and the 10% tax applies to the entire gain, rather than just the amount over €500 ($557).

For example, an investor with €600 ($669) in net gains would face a €60 ($67) tax under the new rate, not €10 ($11). The rules generally calculate gains by subtracting the acquisition cost from the value received when the crypto is disposed of.

Selling Bitcoin for euros is one taxable event, but spending crypto on goods or services can also count as a disposal. Investors will need transaction records to establish what they paid for their assets and what those assets were worth when sold or spent.

Yield from staking, lending, and providing liquidity to DeFi protocols is treated differently. Greece will classify those returns as interest income and tax them at 15%, according to the Finance Ministry.

Qualifying crypto losses exceeding €500 ($557) can be carried forward for five years and offset against future crypto gains. The bill also gives investors a 12-month window to voluntarily declare previously unreported crypto income without late-filing penalties or interest, provided they meet its conditions and pay within 60 days of filing.

The passage follows an October public consultation on the proposal.The draft proposed a 10% rate that was already lower than the 15% levy Greek officials had discussed in June.

Athens says exchanging one cryptocurrency for another should not itself trigger a capital-gains tax. But the wording lawmakers approved may not make that exclusion as clear as the government intended.

A review by parliament's Scientific Service flagged the bill's broad language covering disposals made "for consideration." That phrase could include token-to-token trades, the review said, even though the accompanying explanation says such swaps should not create taxable gains.

The reviewers recommended an explicit exemption and suggested clearer rules for exchanges involving stablecoins and electronic-money tokens. Those details matter for traders who rotate between assets without cashing out to fiat currency.

Greece is also preparing the administrative rules needed to put the tax into practice. A senior Finance Ministry official told Reuters that guidance on transaction transparency and tax audits would be issued in the coming weeks.

The government has not estimated how much the tax will raise. Officials say the market is difficult to measure because many Greek crypto investors use trading platforms based overseas.

#5016. Treasury Cuts Crypto Exchange MEXC Off From US Financial System

Treasury Cuts Crypto Exchange MEXC Off From US Financial System

The U.S. Treasury Department has moved to sever crypto exchange MEXC Global from the American financial system, accusing it of facilitating at least $16 billion in illicit transactions tied to Iran, North Korea and Russia.

In an order issued Friday through its Financial Crimes Enforcement Network, Treasury said MEXC, one of the world’s 20 largest crypto exchanges, served as a major conduit for sanctions evasion and cybercrime.

The action, part of a broader effort dubbed Operation Economic Outcast, bars U.S. financial institutions from processing any funds transfers to or from the exchange. It takes effect 30 days after publication in the Federal Register.

“Financial institutions cannot have it both ways: Either you support the Iranian regime, or you have access to the U.S. financial system,” Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said in a statement, vowing to keep severing the regime’s “economic lifelines” and its attempts to use digital assets to dodge sanctions.

Founded in 2018, MEXC processes more than $1 billion in daily transactions, holds around $5 billion in reserves, and offers more than 3,000 tokens, according to FinCEN. Between May 2021 and May 2026, FinCEN said, the platform moved at least $16 billion connected to illicit activity, including nearly $1.5 billion tied to Russian sanctions evasion, with $232 million in direct transactions with sanctioned entities. The agency named MEXC a “primary money laundering concern” in connection with Russian illicit finance.

Despite publicly claiming anti-money-laundering controls and barring users in places like Iran and North Korea, MEXC’s compliance was riddled with “severe deficiencies,” FinCEN said.

The exchange was still transacting with sanctioned Russian exchange Garantex and its successor Grinex as recently as June 2026, more than four years after Garantex was first designated. It also processed roughly $200 million linked to the A7A5 stablecoin and about $22 million benefiting Iran’s central bank and its Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps.

Those connections place MEXC squarely within a sprawling sanctions-evasion ecosystem that Treasury has been dismantling piece by piece.

The department last month designated Russia’s A7 Network a transnational criminal organization and proposed cutting its front companies off from U.S. finance, flagging more than $179 billion in transactions through its ruble-backed A7A5 token, much of it routed through Garantex and Grinex.

The same campaign has reached into Iran’s crypto sector, where Treasury gained sweeping authority to sanction anyone operating in it and blacklisted the Iranian exchange Nobitex over its role in funneling crypto to the IRGC.

The order uses a provision of the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act rather than a standard sanctions designation, a tool FinCEN has increasingly reached for to cut off crypto infrastructure that operates outside the correspondent banking system.

#5015. Bitcoin ETFs shed $1.3B in biggest weekly outflow since late June

Investors pulled a net $1.21 billion from BlackRock’s IBIT last week as Bitcoin fell below $76,000 and US technology stocks sold off.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) recorded $1.28 billion in net outflows last week, their largest weekly withdrawals since late June.

The withdrawals brought the funds’ net outflows for the year to about $919 million, according to SoSoValue. The funds had already posted $515 million in net outflows in October.

Bitcoin fell below $76,000 last week as traders digested the fallout from Binance’s $6.3 billion hack, uncertainty after the US midterm elections and a sell-off in technology stocks.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT), the world’s largest spot Bitcoin ETF by assets, recorded $1.21 billion in net outflows last week, according to Farside Investors data.

The withdrawals extended IBIT’s losing streak to six consecutive trading sessions, with approximately $1.44 billion pulled from the fund since Oct. 30. IBIT accounted for about 95% of the weekly total reported by SoSoValue.

IBIT recorded net outflows for six consecutive trading sessions through Friday, bringing withdrawals since Oct. 30 to about $1.44 billion.

Fidelity’s Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) drew about $93 million in net inflows for the week, despite losing nearly $200 million Thursday.

Shifting expectations for US interest rates, uncertainty after the midterms and weakness in technology stocks weighed on crypto markets, Bitfire Research said in a statement shared with Cointelegraph.

Bitfire said investors were also reassessing whether Congress could pass the CLARITY Act before year-end.

Related: Bitcoin price dips under $75K as crypto markets miss tech-stock rebound

Bitfire cited a Crypto Fear & Greed Index reading of 25 and a negative Coinbase Premium, which indicates Bitcoin was trading at a discount on Coinbase relative to the indicator’s benchmark.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index. Source: Alternative.me

Bitfire described the market as being in a “breakdown and bottom-seeking phase,” pointing to upcoming US inflation data and signals from the Federal Reserve as factors to watch. It said that a sustained break below the $74,400–$75,000 support range could lead to further selling.

Bitcoin traded at $74,049 at publication, below the lower end of that range. It was down 5% over seven days and 2.3% over 24 hours, according to CoinGecko.

#5014. This Is Not Your Ordinary Flu Season

The CDC released new data that changes what to expect this season.

EVERY FLU SEASON is different. Some come earlier, others later. Some are mild, while others—like last year—barrel through like a tornado. Every year, scientists do their best to predict what type of influenza virus will hit the US based on data from countries in the Southern Hemisphere whose flu seasons come earlier than ours.

The key word here is predict. Since the influenza virus mutates quickly, it’s hard for scientists to give a 100 percent guarantee that what’s ravaging Australia will also come for the US. That’s what happened in the brutal flu season last year—and this year.

A few weeks ago, scientists gave a forecast of H3N2 (that’s the one dubbed “Super-K”) as the dominant flu strain for the US. But early surveillance in flu cases from the West Coast found that a different type—Influenza A virus subtype H1N1—has overtaken H3N2 as the dominant flu strain in October. The CDC also released new flu data reporting a spike in flu-related hospitalizations.

Does H1N1 sound eerily familiar? H1N1 is also known as swine flu—a strain that sparked a pandemic back in 2009. So far, researchers have linked early flu ER visits to a mutation in H1N1 (a G155E mutation) that changes the way the virus attaches to and infects cells. Past research has found antibodies from past H1N1 infections or vaccinations may not recognize it, especially in younger populations. In other words, there is a chance the mutated influenza virus could evade some pre-existing immunity.

Here is what you need to know about this early jump to flu season and how to best protect yourself against this mutated strain.

HERE’S WHAT THE CDC learned: In mid-October this year, the agency saw 10 times more hospitalizations for flu than it did in 2025 (2.1 hospitalizations per 100,000 people compared to 0.2 per 100,000 during the same week last year).

Among other changes are when and where the virus is spreading. While influenza cases typically tick up around fall, infections began rising in August. The boost of early cases was mostly on the West Coast, including Arizona, Nevada, and California. Hawaii and Louisiana are already at moderate levels. Typically, the US flu season starts in the South.

Flu activity remains low nationally. But as of October 28, the CDC estimates that infections are growing or likely growing in 39 states. The numbers coincide with early reports of flu activity ramping up on the East Coast in states including Pennsylvania and New York.

WHILE SCIENTISTS INITIALLY predicted a “Super-K” flu strain to be dominant, this year’s vaccine accounts for multiple flu strains. The current flu shots will target three strains: H1N1, B/Victoria lineage, and H3N2. It’s too soon to see if the vaccine will fully protect against the mutated H1N1 strain, but it’s still expected to provide some meaningful protection.

“A mismatch is not a black-or-white situation. We may have less immunity, not no immunity,” Andrew Pekosz, PhD, virologist at the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health told TODAY.com. You can also take some reassurance from last year when people who still got the mismatched flu shot were still less likely to have severe illness and be hospitalized than those who were unvaccinated.

ALONG WITH YOUR traditional flu shot, adults ages 50 and over are eligible for a new flu vaccine called mFLUSIVA made with the same mRNA technology as the COVID vaccines. A phase 3 clinical trial on the mFLUSIVA vaccine found it was more effective at preventing illness from influenza virus subtypes A and B than a standard vaccine. Reminder, the dominant strain right now is Influenza A virus subtype H1N1.

Of the 20,179 people injected with the new shot, only 411 developed a lab-confirmed case (2 percent) 14 days after vaccination during flu season last year. Meanwhile, 557 of 20,124 recipients of the standard vaccine got sick with the flu (2.8 percent).

One caveat, however, was that the new flu vaccine has a greater chance of severe side effects. In the clinical trial, people were more likely to report injection site pain, fatigue, headaches, and muscle weakness.

Don’t want a shot? That’s fine! You also have your choice of a nasal flu vaccine a.k.a. FluMist if you’re ages 2 to 49. “We tend to develop less of an immune response in the nose as we get older, which means the nasal spray vaccine doesn’t work as well in older groups,” John Sellick, DO, infectious disease expert and professor of medicine at the University at Buffalo, previously told MH.

Ultimately, time isn’t running out yet to get yourself armed and ready for the US flu season. The influenza virus is fast and mutates easily, but vaccination remains your best option to weather the storm.

#5011. Sony Axing Game Discs Will Have Outsized Impact on Low-Income Gamers, Experts Say

Sony Axing Game Discs Will Have Outsized Impact on Low-Income Gamers, Experts Say

Gamers in low-income areas will be among the hardest hit by Sony’s decision to stop producing physical game discs from 2028, experts told Decrypt, while one African games industry expert arguing the continent stands to lose out badly.

The secondary market has long been a cheap way for gamers to get hold of games. The PlayStation Store does run sales, but buying digitally assumes a decent internet connection, which rural households are less likely to have.

"By abandoning physical discs, some players at the bottom of the economic spectrum lose access to physical distribution through retail and, more importantly, discounted used sales," Joost van Dreunen, professor at the NYU Stern School of Business and video game business author, told Decrypt. "It won't immediately show up in the financials of major game makers, since their focus is now on creating premium experiences for a luxury class of gamers."

It comes as the cost of being a gamer is trending upwards. Sony raised PS5 prices in March, for example, citing "pressures in the global economic landscape." Similarly, the next generation of Xbox is expected to cost up to $1,800, according to Fortune.

A spokesperson for UK advocacy group Gamer's Voice told Decrypt that Sony's move to cut discs will "likely affect people on lower incomes more."

Isa Muhammad, founder of Africa and Middle East-focused games industry platform Bijetic, told Decrypt that the PS5 is already "out of reach for most Africans." He stressed that those who can buy a console still rely on the second-hand games market to afford to play on the consoles.

Well, at least the highest-income Africans could still take advantage of the PlayStation Store discounts, right? Unfortunately not. Muhammad said that the PlayStation Network is only supported in one African nation: South Africa. Many other nations are missing from the PlayStation Network's official list of supported countries.

"A gamer in Nigeria who wants to buy digitally has to set up an account registered in the U.S. or UK, then pay with foreign gift cards or a dollar card," Muhammad said.

Download speeds in rural, remote, and low-income areas are another major hurdle.

"Bandwidth is now part of what a game costs," van Dreunen said. "Roughly one in four rural [American] households has no wired provider offering 100/20 Mbps, and at 10 Mbps a 50GB download takes about 11 hours."

In Africa and the Middle East, Muhammad said, internet speeds vary significantly across the region—from super-fast connections in Dubai to unreliable ones in Iran. Internet access is also costly in some countries.

"Just recently I paid 75,000 NGN (around $55) for a 450GB broadband bundle, which is more than Nigeria's minimum monthly wage," Muhammad told Decrypt. With GTA 6's pre-load file expected to be 212GB, "a gamer here would first spend $80 on the game, then anywhere from $25 to over $60 just to download it, depending on how they buy data."

And at Nigeria's median download speed of 36.77 Mbps, per Speedtest by Ookla, the download would take nearly 13 hours. Not to mention the fact that they'd need to have a non-Nigerian PlayStation account, a download code corresponding to the country their account is supposedly from, and a way to pay in the local currency.

"Sony's own terms say you must register your account in the country where you live, and Sony can suspend or close accounts created with false information," Muhammad told Decrypt. "So every Nigerian or Egyptian on a US or UK PSN account is breaking the rules from day one."

African gamers live in fear of needing help from Sony and being refused it (or worse, banned) because their accounts break its rules, Muhammad explained. In a future where every purchase is digital, it could open the door to gamers losing accounts that hold every game they've ever purchased.

"If a player in Africa buys GTA 6 and can't recover their account, they may have no choice but to buy it again on a new one, along with everything else they've lost," he said. "A disc-free future will see this become the reality for every new PlayStation game, for gamers across most of Africa."

Sony and PlayStation did not respond to Decrypt's request for comment.

#5010. New Camping Gear We’re Testing (and Liking) This Fall

Rainproof apparel, heated pads for the tent, and equipment for the fall camping season.

HELLO, SHOULDER SEASON. Easily one of the best and (sometimes) overlooked times for camping, fall has all kinds of intrigue. From fewer people and colorful foliage to a crispness to the air and the possibility of high-elevation snow, there’s so much to be drawn to.

Sure, the temperature will fall, but that’s no reason to stow away the sleeping bag and tent for the year. Instead, add a few pieces of gear here and there to set yourself up for the best fall camping outing imaginable. That could be anything from a proper car camping mattress to another layer of apparel. Of course, a little waterproof gear and some added light-producers will only help your cause.

So, keep that camp stove within reach as we slip into November and December. There’s a decent chance your favorite campsite is still open and better still, not subject to a waiting list.

#5009. The 10 Best Underwear for Women, Tested by a Style Writer

Our favorite pair on this list is 100% cotton.

As a style writer who tests clothing for a living, I’ve worn my fair share of underwear—from moisture-wicking underwear and leakproof underwear to workout underwear that disappears under leggings. And let me tell you: not all panties are made the same. After years of trial and error, I’ve narrowed it down to the ones that actually live up to their claims.

The options on this list are super soft, comfortable, and flattering under clothes, with a variety of fits to choose from. Most are thongs since they’re the most versatile, vanishing under clothing with minimal chafing. This pair is also one of our favorite underwear for working out because it has a barely-there feel. The waistband doesn’t ride up, fall down, or dig in, which can sometimes be an issue with bikini-style undies. From gentle yoga flows to intense interval training, it moves with you through every bend and sprint.

For daily wear, I love a pair of 100% cotton underwear, and this one is one of the comfiest pairs I’ve tested. But it’s important to remember that cotton isn’t always the best choice for working out, says fabric expert Cathy Rust. That’s because (while it is super breathable), once it gets wet, it doesn’t dry out as quickly as other materials. Though if you prioritize natural fabrics for your underwear, this is a great pick.

#5008. AI Chip Stocks Bounce After Global Rout—But Is the Bubble Starting to Burst?

AI Chip Stocks Bounce After Global Rout—But Is the Bubble Starting to Burst?

AI stocks staged a comeback Friday after a rout that swept from Wall Street to Asia. But a single green session hardly settles the bigger question for investors heading into the weekend: Is this an overdue correction, or the beginning of an AI bubble bursting?

Nvidia rose 2.78% to $186.64, after plunging 9.3% this Thursday. AMD also bounced to $449.04 following a 17.3% collapse a day earlier. Both stocks remain well below their October peaks, showing how little a sharp relief rally can repair.

It wasn’t just U.S. markets seeing red either. South Korea’s KOSPI lost 6% Friday, while Taiwan’s benchmark dropped more than 8% during the week—its worst week in months. TSMC hit its daily limit-down threshold during the rout. Reuters reported that Taiwanese officials asked state-owned banks to help support prices.

The US Tech 100 cash-market proxy also recovered, gaining 1.85% to 27,007.3, which means it’s down more than 5.5% this week. Its daily candles show the bounce arriving after a much larger slide from October levels, not a return to the previous trend.

The U.S. midterms accelerated fears that Democratic gains would mean tougher scrutiny of data centers. In 40 of 41 local ballot contests on November 3, voters favored limiting development, including by rejecting pro-construction measures, Ballotpedia found. Some votes were advisory, but the political message was unmistakable.

That matters for chip companies because data centers are their biggest growth story. Each project requires vast spending on processors, servers, power and cooling. Restrictions could delay orders or force construction elsewhere.

Yet the doubts predated the election. AI companies and their customers are spending aggressively to build capacity, often before returns are clear. More expensive borrowing makes that bet harder to sustain. Reuters quoted market strategist Steve Sosnick warning that heavily owned stocks were already “priced to perfection.”

A correction means a market has fallen at least 10% from a recent peak. It does not necessarily mean the underlying business is shrinking. Imagine a chipmaker whose shares trade at 60 times annual earnings because investors expect explosive growth. If investors become willing to pay only 40 times those same earnings, the stock can lose a third of its value without profits falling at all.

A bubble bursting is more serious: It happens when expectations prove unrealistic and the money supporting the boom begins to disappear.

History offers a useful warning. During the dot-com crash, the Nasdaq lost roughly 77% between March 2000 and October 2002. The internet was not a fad, but investors had vastly overestimated how quickly companies could turn it into profit.

Telecom operators spent fortunes laying fiber-optic cables for customers who had yet to arrive. Global Crossing, which built an international network, filed for bankruptcy in 2002. The technology survived; many companies and shareholders did not.

AI could face a similar mismatch between long-term promise and near-term returns—and some investors are already talking about it. For example, the RAM shortage is also a business opportunity that many companies are trying to use for their advantage. Former crypto miners are renting their hardware and cloud provider and inference startups are appearing everywhere.

Now, consider a hypothetical company borrowing billions to build a data center packed with Nvidia chips, expecting to rent that computing power at premium prices for years. If customers spend less than anticipated, borrowing becomes more expensive or construction is delayed, the debt still has to be repaid.

The operator may cancel chip orders, its contractors lose work, and utilities that planned new capacity lose anticipated customers. Lenders and investors holding its debt could also take losses. A stock-market selloff can then become a problem for the wider economy.

There is an important difference from 2000, however. Microsoft, Alphabet, Amazon and Meta are profitable businesses with established customers and substantial cash flows, unlike many of the speculative dot-com startups. They can withstand setbacks better than smaller AI-cloud providers that depend on repeatedly borrowing to expand.

It is possible for AI to keep transforming industries while a bunch of individual AI companies fail—and for investors in successful companies to have overpaid.

The financial warning lights are beginning to flicker. Reuters reported Friday that the cost of insuring against a default by AI-cloud provider CoreWeave had climbed above 1,000 basis points. Such protection works somewhat like insurance on a loan: as the price rises, investors are signaling greater concern about repayment.

Yet broader bond markets were still operating relatively normally. That points to growing strain in the riskiest corners of AI financing, not proof of a system-wide crisis.

Next week will offer more concrete tests than another day of red or green candles. Results from CoreWeave, Applied Materials, and Cisco could reveal whether customers are still ordering capacity and equipment, while inflation data will influence expectations for borrowing costs. Stable orders and manageable debt would support the correction argument.

Repeated canceled projects, failed refinancing and shrinking sales would suggest the boom's financial foundations are cracking. The question is not whether AI has a future, but whether every company betting billions on that future can afford to get there.

#5006. Binance, Tether and Trump crypto firms face Senate records deadline

Sen. Richard Blumenthal asked four crypto firms to start sending records by Nov. 30 and warned of further action if they miss the Jan. 4 deadline.

US Senator Richard Blumenthal gave Binance, Tether and two Trump-linked crypto firms until Jan. 4, 2027, to voluntarily turn over records sought in Senate inquiries.

Failure to meet the January deadline could prompt further action, he warned.

Blumenthal, the ranking Democrat and incoming chair of the Senate Permanent Subcommittee on Investigations, sent follow-up letters Thursday to Binance, Tether, World Liberty Financial and Fight Fight Fight LLC, the company that launched President Donald Trump’s TRUMP memecoin.

The letters asked the companies to begin turning over records by Nov. 30. The Jan. 4 deadline falls a day after Democrats are due to take control of the Senate.

Blumenthal wrote that the subcommittee would consider “alternative measures to obtain compliance” if the companies miss the January deadline. The panel’s current rules allow its chair to issue subpoenas for documents and records.

Cointelegraph sought comment from Binance, Tether, World Liberty Financial and Fight Fight Fight LLC but did not receive responses before publication.

Related: US senator probes Tether after reported deal with ex-Trump crypto adviser

In May 2025, Blumenthal opened an inquiry into potential conflicts of interest involving Trump’s crypto ventures. He sought records from both firms on their ownership, foreign investments and safeguards against conflicts of interest. World Liberty Financial’s lawyers disputed his claims and said the company had no affiliation with Fight Fight Fight LLC.

In June 2026, Blumenthal and four other Senate Democrats called for hearings into a reported $500 million deal for an Abu Dhabi investment firm to buy a 49% stake in World Liberty Financial.

In February, Blumenthal asked Binance for records on transactions linked to Iranian entities and Russia’s shadow fleet. Binance called the underlying reports false in its March reply and said that, to its knowledge, no Binance account had transacted directly with an Iran-based entity. In Thursday’s letter, Blumenthal said Binance still hadn’t turned over any of the requested records.

Blumenthal’s letter to Tether renewed requests for records on the use of USDT by sanctioned entities and on Tether’s dealings with Nxum, a consulting firm co-founded by former White House crypto adviser Bo Hines.

In September, Blumenthal released a report by the panel’s Democratic staff alleging that USDT has become a key part of Iran’s shadow banking network. Tether disputed claims that USDT provides a haven for sanctioned actors, saying it helped authorities freeze nearly $550 million in Iran-linked tokens in 2026.

#5005. How to Build Your Legs With the Glute-Ham Raise

This underrated exercise could be the MVP of your lower body training.

IF YOU’RE A dedicated lifter, your lower body doesn’t lack attention in your workouts. You might even dedicate an entire session to it each week (hello, leg day). But just because your training plan is packed with deadlifts, squats, lunges, calf raises, hip thrusts, and step-ups doesn’t mean you’re doing everything you can to maximize your leg development. You probably walk right past one of the most powerful lower body machines every time you’re at the gym: the glute ham developer (GHD).

It might look like a gussied-up horizontal Roman chair, but the GHD is a different beast entirely. Instead of targeting your glutes and lower back with hyperextensions, the GHD allows you to laser focus on your biceps femoris, semitendinosus, and semimembranosus (collectively known as your hamstrings) with an exercise called the glute ham raise (GHR). It’s so effective at what it does that only the deadlift can rival its hamstring-building power, making it a formidable addition to any leg day. Use these tips to weave it into your routine for maximum results.

AS MENTIONED ABOVE, you’ll need a GHD to perform a GHR. Where a Roman chair puts your body at a 45-degree angle and only allows movement in the hips, the GHD elevates you off the floor, locking your ankles between two sets of rollers while your knees (in the starting position) and then thighs (in the down position) rest on rounded pads. In so doing, it shifts the emphasis from your lower back to the backs of your legs, requiring you to use your hamstrings to lower and raise your body primarily through knee flexion as you perform the GHR.

How well does it work? A small research trial suggests the glute ham raise is so effective at targeting your hamstrings that it’s on par with the Romanian deadlift and superior to such leg-sculpting staples as the leg curl and good morning.

And the benefits don’t stop there. In addition to your hams, you’ll light up muscles all along your posterior chain, including your calves, glutes, and erector spinae. Even your abs will kick into gear to help your erector spinae support and stabilize your spine.

IF YOUR STRENGTH program is divided into training splits, add the GHR to your leg day. If your weekly routine skews more toward total body workouts, incorporate the GHR into one or two of them, making sure it’s among a diverse selection of leg exercises spread across three or four strength sessions. Either way, it’s a good idea to perform it toward the end of a workout after heavier compound lifts like the squat and deadlift.

You can load the GHD by holding a weight plate to your chest, but start with only your bodyweight, following the instructions below until you master the movement.

In a true GHR, the lowering phase of the movement stops when your body is horizontal to the floor. Going past that point by hinging downward at your hips until your torso is perpendicular to the floor adds a back extension element and causes you to lose hamstring tension, undercutting your hamstring gains—so make sure you stay disciplined with your range of motion.

*If you don’t have access to a GHD, you can enlist a buddy to hold your ankles on the floor or use a specialized bench to perform a Nordic curl, which also requires your hamstrings to work overtime as you lower and raise your thighs and torso through knee flexion.

Trevor Thieme is a Los Angeles-based writer and strength coach, and a former fitness editor at Men’s Health. When not helping others get in shape, he splits his time between surfing, skiing, hiking, mountain biking, and trying to keep up with his 10-year-old daughter.
